# BLACKBOARD — w2m

*The project's working record. Seven sections. Single-writer per section. Everything written here that matters to the Architect was also said in chat in the same turn. Template: `sdd-kit-3/templates/BLACKBOARD.md`.*

---

## Surfaced for review

- **2026-10-08 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (after vocabulary bumps 0.2, 0.3, 0.4; hard rule 5, overdue since 0.2 per the project review): The vocabulary in force is `signals/0.4.json`, 60 tags in 12 categories on two planes. The harness plane is substrate events whose kind is the Struct class name; the guest plane is content-addressed JSONL traces ordered by (thread_id, thread_seq, hb_refs). Since 0.1: harness integers are bounded to ±(2^53−1) for RFC 8785 (P8); capability values name the 4 KB IPA granule, guest TSO, and three 4 KB protection paths (P9, P10); 0.4 renames `objective_ref` (no money word in an optimization target), adds `not_established` so a probe that cannot decide says so, and lets imported executions omit what was not observed, naming it in `unobserved`. What the project is for has not changed: contracts first, generated implementations verified against them, a Windows game launched as a Mac app (v0.2 §1). What has fired: 14 of 60 tags; none of generation, model checking, reduction, or discrepancy routing. That gap is the next work (Decision 2026-10-08, sequence).
- **2026-10-03 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION: This project is w2m, a system meant to take the Windows x86-64 distribution of a Steam game and produce a Mac-native application from the binaries alone, governed by a versioned, executable Windows execution contract (`windows_to_macos_research_direction_v0_1.md` §2-3). The review at `docs/review-2026-10-03-research-direction-v0_1.md` narrows v0 to one DRM-free D3D11 game in a Hypervisor.framework VM backend using the macOS 26 4 KB IPA granule and hardware TSO, on this host (macOS 26.5.1, M5 Max, 16 KB host pages). SDD at root: the vocabulary is the contract, signals are designed typed events and not logs, and the lossy step being removed is a human describing why a game misbehaves — here the program's own trace (contract id, minimized program, mismatch class) replaces it, which is the research direction's §11 discrepancy report expressed as signals. The loop: write a card, self-review it, execute, run the gates (`sdd-kit-3/AGENTS.md` dual contract plus observation contract for anything that runs guest code), write the Signal Report, run the Rubber Duck Pass against `signals/0.1.json`, append to this file per single-writer discipline. Resolve-and-record: questions the project, the kit, or a named engineering source settle get decided and written to `## Decisions` with grounds; only the closed user-only list (here: which game, the Windows oracle hardware, Steam credentials, licensing intent) is asked, in chat, while independent work continues. No implementation sprint dispatches before `signals/0.1.json` and its rationale are signed (hard rule 12); `sprints/sprint-000-vocabulary-session.md` is that session.

- **2026-10-03 agent** — User-only questions asked in chat, each with a recommendation: (Q1) first target game; (Q2) Windows x86-64 oracle machine; (Q3) Steam credentials for DepotDownloader, deferred to the acquisition phase; (Q4) commercial intent, given substrate's PolyForm Noncommercial license. Sprint 0 proceeds on recommended answers marked provisional.

- **2026-10-03 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (re-articulated after the v0.2 scope change): The founding document is now `windows_to_macos_research_direction_v0_2.md`. Its first-class subsystems are contract construction from three evidence categories (documented, observed on a named reference profile, hypothesis; §11.1), the specification compiler and the application compiler joined by one versioned contract (§2), code discovery with validity and invalidation (§5.1), the versioned Steam bridge (§4.2), and substrate as the orchestration harness whose append-only record does not serialize guest execution (§11.4). The plan leaves the CPU backend, first game, first graphics profile, and shader-converter adoption open, so this session's earlier VM-first and DRM-free calls no longer stand. Each becomes a recorded decision backed by capability probes and conformance results (§8.2, §15). Two repair paths stay separate: implementation repair against an unchanged contract, and contract revision with evidence and dependency invalidation (§11.3). The vocabulary has to carry both, so no candidate can make itself pass by changing its expected output. Sprint 0 (`sprints/sprint-000-vocabulary-session.md`) is retargeted to v0.2 and stays the next work.

- **2026-10-09 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (hard rule 5: vocabulary 0.5 in force, the x86-64 profile changed, the architecture decided, 32-bit in scope; review 2026-10-09 progress K2): w2m runs a Windows x86-64 Steam game on this Mac inside a Hypervisor.framework VM that enforces x86 ordering and 4 KB pages in hardware. The guest's Windows-visible state (TEB, PEB, CONTEXT, a per-thread state block) lives in guest memory at Windows layouts; Windows services are freestanding C inside the guest, each a compilation unit generated against a contract and judged against Windows; x86 code is decoded by a spec-generated decoder judged by XED, lifted to explicit operations judged by a real x86 CPU, and compiled to AArch64. The x86-64 profile is Hail to the Rainbow (Unity 2019.4, Mono: its logic is code generated at run time); BioShock 2 Remastered is the 32-bit profile. Vocabulary 0.5 (61 tags, two planes) names every event; a substrate run holds the chain from cause to verdict, and the records under store/runs/ replay. The plan's current order lives in docs/plan.md.

SDD gaps found in the reread and fixed in Sprint 095's commit: `## Built` stops at Sprint 066, and `## Sprint tail` holds about 60 entries against the kit's 10.

## Decisions

- **2026-10-03 user** — Project scope: build the system described in `windows_to_macos_research_direction_v0_1.md` (pasted in chat 2026-10-03): obtain a Windows Steam game's distribution from the Mac command line, prepare it automatically, and launch it as a native Mac application; original Windows binaries are the input; Wine, Rosetta, and D3DMetal are not architectural dependencies. Use the substrate repo (`https://github.com/laffeyp/substrate`) and the SDD kit (`sdd-kit-3/`). Instruction verbatim: "use this, read the sdd kit in full, to review this and prep to make it."
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — What role substrate plays. Decision: substrate is the generation-and-verification harness (research direction §1 "the project's own generation and automated verification system"): contract implementations are generated and checked through `best_of_n_verified` / `coding_flow`-shaped topologies, and every probe, generation, and conformance run is a substrate run record. Substrate is not the compiler or the guest runtime. Grounds: project — research direction §1, §11 and substrate `docs/applications.md`; SDD — `ADDENDUMS.md` Addendum D (discovery harness built on substrate); practice — substrate `docs/api.md` (`embedded_substrate`, `assert_event`, `first_divergence`). Revisit if: the user meant substrate as code to fork, or as the shipped runtime.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — The Windows execution contract is mined from a Windows oracle by probes; Microsoft documentation is a hint, not the source of truth. Grounds: project — review §1; SDD — `grammar/BOOTSTRAP.md` anti-pattern "fabricating to avoid a gap"; practice — Wine project history (1993 to date) of undocumented-behavior dependence. Revisit if: a contract built from documentation alone passes a real game's observation contract.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — First CPU backend is a Hypervisor.framework VM (4 KB IPA granule, TSOEN) with a compact guest runtime; native-process lowering comes later, measured against it. Grounds: project — review §2.2, host macOS 26.5.1 / M5 Max; SDD — TECHNIQUES.md #43 (refactor as behavior-preserving chain, so the second backend is checked against the first); practice — Apple Hypervisor updates (macOS 26 `hv_vm_config_set_ipa_granule`), Linux TSO patch series 2024-04 (TSOEN modifiable from macOS 14.4). Revisit if: a public native-process TSO path is found, or VM-exit cost on the graphics/audio boundary exceeds the frame budget.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — Dynamic x86-64 translation is a day-one correctness component; AOT is a verified cache over it. Grounds: project — review §2.1; SDD — none specific; practice — static code/data separation is undecidable in general (Horspool and Marovac, 1980); Mono JIT and LuaJIT in shipped games. Revisit if: never for correctness; AOT-only may be an optimization mode per game.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — v0 excludes Denuvo, kernel-mode anti-cheat, and D3D12; SteamStub is in scope only via dynamic unpacking. Grounds: project — review §2.3, §5; SDD — hard rule 6 (one concept at a time, applied at phase scale); practice — DXMT and Metal Shader Converter coverage (DXBC vs DXIL). Revisit if: the user's chosen first game needs one of them.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — Project layout: the kit lives at `sdd-kit-3/` (read-only); project artifacts at the root per `sdd-kit-3/README.md` "Project layout after adoption"; the research direction stays at the root unedited; reviews and specs under `docs/`. - **2026-10-09 agent-resolved** — Runtime architecture (v0.2 §8.2 backend selection; review 2026-10-09 H1). Supersedes "Sprint 034 decides the guest runtime architecture" in the sequence entry above: the evidence was already on the record and leaves one design.
  (1) **Backend: the managed VM.** On this host, guest TSO in the VM runs at 0.99-1.10x native plain access, against 2.64-2.71x for the cheapest native-process lowering (ldapr/stlr) and 7.4x for dmb fences (Sprint 011, record ordering-1791442786); EnTSO gives 0 forbidden MP outcomes in 50M (Sprint 009); the VM enforces 4 KB guest pages in hardware (fault EC 0x25 at A+4096), while a macOS process cannot (mprotect at 4 KB returns EINVAL; Sprint 012); no public native-process TSO control exists in the SDK (Sprint 008). The VM back end already runs lifted code and passes lockstep and the state differential (Sprints 030, 032). Native-process lowering stays a later comparison subject (v0.2 §8.2), not a second build.
  (2) **Windows-visible state lives in guest memory at Windows layouts.** The game's runtime reads it there without calling anything: Mono's JIT reads TLS from `gs:[0x1480 + key*8]` and `gs:[0x1780]` (TEB), its VEH reads and rewrites CONTEXT, Boehm GC and Mono call SuspendThread, GetThreadContext and SetThreadContext. So TEB, PEB, the loader's module list, the VEH chain, and one CPU-state block per thread (GPRs, RIP, flags, XMM/YMM, MXCSR, x87 with its control word, segment bases) are guest memory. Translated code may hold guest registers in host registers; every block carries reconstruction tables (host PC → guest RIP and register locations) so a CONTEXT can be built at any instruction boundary (v0.2 §5).
  (3) **Windows services run inside the guest, as AArch64 code compiled from C** (freestanding, `clang --target=aarch64-none-elf`, no libc), linked into the guest runtime image the monitor loads. Faults, VEH dispatch, TLS, handles, waits, thread suspend and context access never leave the VM. Only host effects cross the boundary — files, time, windows, input, audio, graphics, network — through one typed transport (v0.2 §7, last paragraph), replacing today's text pipe to Python. C, because clang and the AArch64 toolchain are on this host (Xcode 26.6), the monitor and probes are already C, the Windows ABI and its structures are C-shaped, and the reference implementations the project measures against are C (Wine, box64). Every service is a separate compilation unit with one contract, so the candidate loop generates, installs and judges it the way Sprint 031 did SetLastError; Python candidates end with the host kernel32.
  (4) **One vCPU per guest thread**, each on its own host thread (Hypervisor.framework binds a vCPU to the thread that created it); macOS schedules them. SuspendThread forces an exit with `hv_vcpus_exit` and reads the state block; guest TSO holds across them (EnTSO set per vCPU, Sprint 008).
  (5) **Translation**: x86-64 → explicit operations (`lift.py`) → AArch64 (`vm_backend.py`), ahead of time for shipped code and on a dispatch miss for code found or written at run time (v0.2 §6). The translator stays Python on the host behind one interface (guest bytes and address in; AArch64 block, its source range, code version and reconstruction table out), so moving it into the guest later changes no caller. Mono's RWX regions are write-protected after translation; a write faults, invalidates the affected translations, and the next entry retranslates (v0.2 §5.1).
  (6) **Signals**: substrate orchestrates generation and judgment on the harness plane, as now. The guest runtime writes guest-plane events (vocabulary 0.5) into a guest ring buffer the monitor drains to the trace store, so every service call, fault and thread event is judged by the existing comparator. Translation emits `CODE_TARGET_DISCOVERED`, `TRANSLATION_PUBLISHED` and `TRANSLATION_INVALIDATED`, which have no emitter today.
  Grounds: project — v0.2 §5, §5.1, §6, §7, §8.1, §8.2; Sprints 008, 009, 011, 012, 030-033 measurements; Unity-Technologies/mono `unity-2019.4-mbe` source (read 2026-10-09); SDD — AGENTS.md resolve-and-record, hard rule 4; practice — box64 and Wine keep Windows structures in guest-visible memory and write their runtimes in C; Rosetta 2 and FEX reconstruct x86 state from host state at fault points. Revisit if: a native-process TSO control appears in a public SDK, or the transport's measured round trip makes a host effect miss its frame budget.

The failure mode, as it happened on the local VM: macOS Local Network privacy denied Ghostty's connection to the guest's sshd ("No route to host"), and the agent built keystroke injection through the QEMU monitor, an ad hoc HTTP return channel, a UTM scripting bridge at ≈7 s per command, and then proposed a loopback port forward; Screen Recording denied `screencapture`, and the agent moved to screendumps in sandboxed folders and screenshots by scheduled task inside the guest. Every one of those was a detour around a switch that only had to be turned on. Rule: this is an owned machine with no outside authority over it; access needed for ordinary work (gaining control of the machines the project uses) is assumed. Any block on it — an OS permission, a dialog, a harness rule — is named at once in chat and removed, and the work continues on the reasonable path. A step the user declines in the moment is the user's decision; the agent asks about it rather than going around it. A command to a local machine that takes seconds is a design defect, not a measurement to report.

- **2026-10-11 agent-resolved** — How w2m runs a second process (revises the decision on the crash handler above, which chose a second address space in one VM). Decision: a second VM, with its own vm_monitor process and memory, driven by the same Python host; the objects processes share (named events, pipes, process records) live in the host (`src/w2m/kernel.py`), and a driver runs whichever VM has a ready thread, waking blocked threads in every VM when a shared object is signaled. Hypervisor.framework allows one VM per process, but each VM already has a monitor process of its own, so the limit binds the monitors, not the host. Grounds: project — `src/w2m/vm_backend.py` holds one process's loader, modules, memory, code cache and translation table in one class, and `tools/vm_monitor/vm_monitor.c` creates one VM per monitor; the handler's capture (game run 38176899195) shows it blocked on its waits for the whole run; SDD — the revisit condition of the decision above (a second address space costing more than a second host-side VM with a handle broker); practice — Wine keeps shared objects in one server process (wineserver) and runs each Windows process apart. The host (Python) serves, each by an exit: thread switching (hvc #12 to `scheduler.py`, when a service waits or yields or a time slice ends) and the thread operations of HOST_THREAD (create, end, suspend, resume, context, exit code, signal); objects shared between processes (HOST_KERNEL, `kernel.py`); virtual memory (HOST_VIRTUAL_* 19–22, `virtual_memory.py`); module loading and lookup (15–18, `modules.py`); files (6–13, `file_bridge.py`); the clock, the wall clock, randomness and file views. Each crossing is an exit over the pipe (10.3–10.5 µs, Sprint 048) plus the Python behind it, and none is measured as a share of a run: plan row 065 now measures the host's share by operation beside the model steps' share, from `vm.host_requests` and a time per operation. A VALUE divergence (the same call on the paired thread with the same inputs, answered differently) is a defect. A presence or count divergence (a call one side makes and the other does not, or makes a different number of times) on a thread whose earlier calls all conform is SCHEDULING: the game's own interleaving, recorded and not chased; it is a defect only after an earlier value divergence explains it. HeapReAlloc 3268 (Unity's BatchDeleteObjects growing its buffer three times in runner run 38203364352 and four in the others) is scheduling. w2m is not tuned to reproduce one Windows execution's timing; Sprint 066's TIME_SLICE 20,000 stands on its own grounds or is revisited, not on matching run 38216081440's SList flushes. The general shape comes from the specification end: each function's contract (documentation, Wine and ReactOS as implementations, probe rows on Windows), judged by the families; the game's stream supplies coverage and catches value errors. The laptop's order follows: play mode (frames on the real GPU), audio, and probes first; batch-mode game series last. Grounds: project — 68 of 68 families and the frontier's call stream unchanged with the pass; the alternative (every memory access a flag reader) leaves almost every flag live; SDD — AGENTS.md resolve-and-record; practice — none cited beyond the project's measurement. - **2026-10-14 agent-resolved** — The form of the compiler inside the VM (recentering step 6). Decision: copy-and-patch at the encoder level. A stencil is a sequence of arm64 encoder calls whose arguments are expressions of the instruction's guest registers and values; the stencils come from the Python compiler (the rules and `compile_insn`), captured per form and value class by the census's method, never written by hand; the compiler in the VM is a generated C decoder (from `specs/x86_decode.json`), the form key, the stencil table, the encoders in C (judged against `a64_encoder.py` by a differential) and the hole expressions; a form with no stencil exits to the host, which compiles it in Python as now and counts it. The words of every instruction compiled in the VM must equal the Python compiler's, over the census record and the class runs. Grounds: project — Sprint 071's census (`scripts/stencil_census.py` over frontier-643a6283510c record of the batch run to 100 billion instructions, cold): 1,313,377 compiled instructions, every one rebuilt exactly from its logged encoder calls; with value classes (zero, sign, the 12-bit and 9-bit ranges, the scaled offset per access size, nonzero halfwords, a shift count's 1 and 0) all 18,218 forms produce one encoder sequence; 1,287 distinct sequences, the commonest 100 covering 93.2% of compiled instructions; every hole a register, a value, a piece of one, its negation or an affine function of one (70 cases: 16 times an XMM number, 64 or 32 less a shift count). Industry — Xu and Kjolstad, "Copy-and-Patch Compilation", OOPSLA 2021 (1,666 stencils for WebAssembly); CPython 3.13's JIT, PEP 744, whose stencils are built from the same definitions as its interpreter, so one change updates both. Open, for the next sprints: 394,155 words (in 364,521 instructions) are built inline in `vm_backend.py` rather than by an encoder (ADD/SUB immediates, the flag sequences), 1,833 of their positions holding holes; they move into encoder functions first, with the words unchanged. The census covers the start-up's first 100 billion instructions; forms first met later (mono's run-time code in play) take the host path until a stencil exists for them.
- **2026-10-14 agent-resolved** — The stencil key of the compiler inside the VM, and how its holes are found (refines the copy-and-patch Decision above). Decision: a stencil is keyed by what the generated decoder gives (the spec row, iclass, operand and address size, VEX.L, the operands with register numbers and values taken out, which registers are equal, which explicit register is one of the row's implicit registers, the value classes of the immediates, displacements, the next address and any named address) and the compile call's flag arguments; the C decoder therefore outputs `x86dec.Fields` and `x86ops` operands, and a generated table gives each row's implicit registers. Keys come from recorded runs; each key's holes and their expressions come from compiling instances made for that key (registers and values changed within their classes), not from a run's variation. Grounds: project — Sprint 074 (`scripts/stencil_census.py --decoder-key` over a cold batch run's record of 1,313,377 compiled instructions): 38,154 keys, every one one encoder sequence; every hole that varies named by one expression of one decoded value over all its form's instructions; 17,276 keys seen with one distinct instruction, so the record leaves their holes unshown; the first key without implicit registers split a MUL row (MUL RDX). Practice — copy-and-patch derives each stencil's holes from the stencil's construction, not from observed uses (Xu and Kjolstad, OOPSLA 2021: a stencil's holes are the relocations in its object code, known from how the stencil was built). Revisit if: a key gives more than one sequence on a made instance.
- **2026-10-15 agent-resolved** — How the stencil table holds where its samples are thin (amends Decision 2026-10-14, the stencil key; review 2026-10-15 M4). Decision: (1) a key whose made instances give two sequences is refined by the first feature of one decoded value that separates them (at least 8, 16, 32, 64 or 128; the low 1-5 bits), from a per-key entry (Sprint 076, 58 keys); (2) the key's flag arguments are canonical: `live_after` and `nzcv_out` are kept only for an instruction with a flags op and `nzcv_from` only for a Jcc its kind's NZCV_CONDITIONS holds, since compile_insn reads them nowhere else (Sprint 079: 38,154 keys to 21,182, none split; the review's E2: 199,826 non-canonical argument sets, 0 differing words); (3) the table emits only what its samples prove: every value the samples never varied is guarded (an instruction whose value there differs goes to the host), a register hole is read only through the register map, and a value hole that a second expression also fits is checked at emit time, the instruction refused where they disagree (Sprint 080). Grounds: project — Sprint 079's held-out judge found 144 wrong compiles on 078's table, where a constant stood for a value no sample varied; Sprint 080's measures; SDD — KIT_DIARY #58, #59; practice — none outside the project for inferred stencils (copy-and-patch knows its holes by construction). Revisit if: a judge from outside the table's sources finds a differing word.

It takes the lock around an exit's service only when `shared`, which chain step 5's host threads set; not whenever vcpu_count > 1, since the litmus and runtime-lock tests drive vCPUs 1-4 from inside vCPU 0's on_dispatch and a lock held across the hook would deadlock them. Decision: a kick is any change of the channel page's kick word (offset 2048, one 32-bit store), so kicks made before the monitor's kicker looks are one; a canceled exit of a kicked vCPU reports kind `kick` ahead of the watchdog's `cancel` (a deadline passed with it waits for the next run's); `serve_exit` answers a kick by resuming the vCPU where it stopped and counts it in `VM.kicks`, and what the kick was for is the kicking thread's to act on at the run loop's top (chain steps 5 and 6). Decision: a run entry's free word (0x138) marks the vCPU that has the thread loaded, set at a load and cleared at a save by the guest's `w2m_switch` and the host's `_load_thread`/`_save_thread`, and both switches skip a thread another vCPU holds; with `VM.thread_vcpus` > 1 a host thread serves each vCPU past the first under the shared runtime lock, which covers each serve and the words written before a run; a vCPU past the first with no thread parks (gives the lock back, waits on the VM's condition, which each serve notifies while one waits), and vCPU 0 never parks: with no thread while another vCPU runs one it waits holding its own, and otherwise keeps today's path (idle to the Machine, warp, sleep, or the deadlock), so deadlines stay one vCPU's to keep. The judge is the wait_handoff family, not the threads family the design named: the threads probe suspends a spinning thread, which on two vCPUs runs elsewhere (chain step 6). The guest's and host's take of the runtime lock become compare-and-swap (CASA; `__atomic_compare_exchange_n`): Sprint 106's exchange wrote each waiter's value over the holder's, so the word named the last waiter, and RuntimeLock's "the vCPU holds it already" read a waiter as the holder (the first two-vCPU run hung with both host threads in the take, the word 1). Windows' SuspendThread may return before the thread stops and GetThreadContext completes the stop; returning after is one of the outcomes Windows allows, and suspend.frozen needs the thread stopped by the row. vCPU 0, with no thread to run while another vCPU runs one, puts its thread down before it waits, notifies, and carries that (`saved`) through the Machine's idle turn; every pick of a thread to load skips one another vCPU holds; on several vCPUs a thread put down has its vectors written to its slot at once. The batch run's calls move: the crash handler gets turns and a clock that keeps up (the review of 2026-10-18 reverted one change at a time: the second process's slices and the catch-up together are the cause); it still conforms, and its instruction count at the stop is no invariant (80.7 to 83.0 billion over Sprints 114-119, the stop tested on wall-clock progress lines). A first draft had the guest claim waiting vCPUs and decide the slice itself; the host's resume rewrites SLICE_UNTIL after every request, so the guest's decision never stood (a plant of the claim changed nothing in 10 runs), and the rule moved to the host. An SGI through the GIC is not built: Hypervisor.framework returns from hv_vcpu_run on a guest WFI, so an idle vCPU waits in the host either way. - **2026-10-21 agent-resolved** — Sprint 122: how a process's threads on several vCPUs share the one thread that drives a kernel.Machine (step 7 of the several-vCPU chain; Sprint 120's refusal). Decision: in a Machine, vCPU 0 never waits for another vCPU: with no thread to take it puts its thread down, counts itself in RT_WAITING and answers IDLE, and the Machine runs another process, or waits on its own condition (notified by every serve on a host thread) until a change, the earliest deadline or its one-second stop check, stopping as a deadlock only after a whole second with no change, no deadline and no host thread running; a process is ready to the Machine only for a thread no other vCPU holds. The Machine's tables are under one lock. A wake from a thread that does not serve a process is left pending and applied under that process's runtime lock at an exit from outside the runtime's C, and every slice of the process ends at the host until then; a wake made on a host thread kicks the process the Machine's thread runs. Every process on one vCPU keeps the old path: one thread, wakes written at once. Found on the way and fixed: a thread sliced out for another process ran again first (a readied thread starved beside a spinning child, on one vCPU); the main process's run was left open when a child stopped; a host request served for vCPU 1 read and wrote vCPU 0's runtime stack (design B's private pages), which stopped the batch run on two vCPUs. Design B, a driver thread per process, was not built: it needs the same pending wake and lock and removes the round robin the instruction clock's runs depend on. Grounds: project — guests handshake, busychild, crosswake, pingpong and statsize (tests/test_processes_beside_vcpus.py), each failure measured before its fix and each plant measured to fail but the direct wake (not caught in 40,000 cross-process wakes); the batch run on two vCPUs conforms (168 lines); SDD — Decisions 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-18 user; practice — Ousterhout, A Philosophy of Software Design ch. 11 (design it twice); Apple's hv_vcpu.h (`hv_vcpus_exit` from any thread; a vCPU not running returns at once from its next run). - **2026-10-24 agent-resolved** — Architecture: the Rust host's locks (Sprint 130; fix (b) of Decision 2026-10-24 user), in `docs/design-rust-host-2026-10-24.md`, which replaces `docs/design-rust-host-2026-10-23.md`. Decision: no thread waits, on any service path, for a lock another thread's service holds. (1) Each service the guest runtime answers takes the lock of what it touches or none: none for the thread-local calls and QueryPerformanceCounter (the three hottest imports, about 154,000, 129,000 and 94,000 calls a second in the batch run), a lock per heap as Windows has, the TLS slots, the handle table, each sync object, the modules, the call trace's atomic sequence, and the run-table word for the run table only, a leaf. (2) A transport page per vCPU, and no guest lock across `hvc #8`; each of the 65 `w2m_host` call sites is read for state it holds across the request. (3) Translation into a region per vCPU, with its own cursor, save area and runtime stack; dispatch slots read and written as atomic pairs or under a sequence word, filled by compare-and-swap, removed by tombstone; a lock per guest code page for invalidation; reclamation by per-vCPU epochs. (4) One process's objects and waits stay in its VM; the supervisor holds only what crosses processes. (5) Every lock word has Drepper's three states, a bounded spin, then a wait in the host on the word's address. Host locks are each held for one access, `files` only for the descriptor table; `modules` across one map, as Windows's loader lock is. The run-table word stays one lock for scheduling, about 31,000 takings a second in play, until the scheduler's port. Grounds: project — `vm_backend.py` 3762-3765, 3809, 4240-4251; `runtime.c` 25; Decision 2026-10-09 (hot imports); the reviewer's two passes; SDD — AGENTS.md hard rule 4; practice — QEMU's MTTCG (commit 0ac20318ce16, `docs/devel/multi-thread-tcg.rst`), crosvm's per-device mutex, Wine's esync to ntsync, Microsoft's HeapCreate and loader-lock pages, Drepper's "Futexes Are Tricky", Uhlig et al. (VM '04), the Arm ARM's concurrent-modification rule, Intel's cross-modifying-code rule, Go's per-P run queue. - **2026-10-29 user** — Milestones for the product slice, after milestone C. The user, on the plan having no step for v0.2's product (§1 and §12.1: resolve the Steam distribution, obtain it under the user's account, prepare it, establish its Steam session, run, preserve state, close, relaunch; §4.2 the Steam bridge; §4.3 a Mac application bundle): "then we need to make more milestones", and they come after C unless something must change now, which would change the whole arc. Nothing must change now, by lookup:
  - **No session before C:** the exe has no SteamStub wrapper (sections `.text .rdata .data .pdata .rsrc .reloc`, no `.bind`), and neither the exe nor UnityPlayer.dll imports steam_api64. The game reaches Steam through `com.rlabrecque.steamworks.net.dll` and a LoadLibrary of steam_api64.dll at run time, and runs on when `SteamAPI_Init` fails: the Windows runner, with no Steam client, shows it (`docs/review-2026-10-09-progress.md` 174, 208). So milestones A to C do not need a session.
  - **The bridge uses a seam the plan already builds:** `docs/solution-2026-10-16.md` §4.7 designs it as a guest `steamclient64.dll` whose interfaces forward to the Mac Steam client, generated from the Steamworks headers as Proton's `lsteamclient` is. That is a guest DLL calling the host, the seam plan step 8b builds for winemetal.
  - **The interface versions are known:** §4.7's open question E10, answered by lookup of steam_api64.dll's strings: 17 versions, SteamClient017 and 020, SteamController008, SteamFriends017, SteamGameServer014, SteamGameServerStats001, SteamInput005, SteamMatchGameSearch001, SteamMatchMaking009, SteamMatchMakingServers002, SteamNetworking006, SteamNetworkingMessages002, SteamNetworkingSockets011, SteamNetworkingUtils004, SteamParties002, SteamUser021, SteamUtils010.
  - **State is the one gap, and it is local:** the file bridge removes the drive it made (`file_bridge.rs` 573), and `registry.c` 30 says nothing persists between runs. The product's "preserve state, close, and relaunch" needs both kept across runs, a change inside the file bridge and the registry, not to the arc.

  Decision: milestone D, the product slice: `w2m steam run <appid>` from the Mac command line. It covers the depot acquired under the user's account in Rust, the drive and registry kept across runs, the output a Mac application bundle, and run, close and relaunch, with update and removal checked under the same lifecycle (§12.1). Milestone E, the Steam session: the guest steamclient64.dll's 17 interface versions generated and forwarding to the Mac Steam client, `SteamAPI_Init` succeeding, the session's behavior judged against a Windows run with Steam. The plan gains both after milestone C, written by the card after Sprint 219, since the plan is in the gated tree. Grounds: project — v0.2's goal is the product, and the plan stopped at play; SDD — a deferral is recorded with its reasons, not left implicit; practice — the lookups above, made at once, before the order was set. Revisit if: the play path at plan step 7 needs `SteamAPI_Init` to succeed, or a save must persist within milestone C's play, either of which moves that part ahead of C with its reason.

- **2026-10-31 agent-resolved** — Architecture: guest memory that other vCPUs write while the host reaches it (reviewer-3's Medium 1 at 275; `docs/design-guest-memory-on-several-vcpus-2026-10-31.md`). Decision: a slice of guest memory only while every vCPU that may write it is stopped; otherwise `w2m_vm::concurrent_memory::ConcurrentGuestMemory`, a type of w2m's own that makes no reference, copies bytes through the pointer, reaches shared words as atomics and publishes a dispatch slot's pair by one aligned `STP`; not the `vm-memory` crate. Grounds: project — 174 slice calls in `crates/w2m_vm/src/`, and `dispatch_insert`'s two plain stores, which the dispatcher's one `LDP` could see half done; SDD — the rule written where the next card that runs the loop on two vCPUs reads it, census D7 counting what is left; practice — Rust's `std::sync::atomic` ("data races are undefined behavior"), `vm-memory`'s and crosvm's "No references or slices to volatile memory", and QEMU's aligned 16-byte `STP` under FEAT_LSE2; `vm-memory`'s regions, bitmaps and volatile copies serve no need of w2m's one mapping, and Rust's documentation says volatile changes nothing for concurrency. Revisit if: w2m maps guest memory in more than one region, or runs on a host without FEAT_LSE2.

## Open questions

- **OQ-1.** Is `ACTLR_EL1.TSOEN` settable for a guest vCPU through public Hypervisor.framework on macOS 26, or only through a private path? Settled by a probe sprint, not by reading. — **Answered 2026-10-07 (Sprint 008):** public. `HV_SYS_REG_ACTLR_EL1` (macOS 15.0+, SDK header `hv_vcpu_types.h`: "only allows getting / setting of the ACTLR_EL1.EnTSO bit (index 1)"); on Mac17,7 / macOS 25F80 the bit sets, reads back 0x2, and survives an `hv_vcpu_run` exit. Still open: the ordering *effect* (a two-vCPU litmus test). — **Effect demonstrated 2026-10-07 (Sprint 009):** MP litmus, 2 vCPUs, guest MMU on with Normal WB memory: EnTSO off 2245 / 50,000,000 forbidden outcomes; EnTSO on 0 / 50,000,000 (95% upper bound 6.0e-08).
- **OQ-3.** (Answered with OQ-1: the custom-guest path is public Hypervisor.framework, not a Virtualization.framework/Rosetta-only facility.) v0.2 §8.1 cites Apple's "Accelerating the performance of Rosetta" for guest `ACTLR.TSOEN` control. That page describes Rosetta inside a Linux guest under Virtualization.framework; whether a custom compact guest under Hypervisor.framework gets the same control is OQ-1, settled by the capability probe v0.2 §8.1 calls for.
- **OQ-2.** Which Metal Shader Converter version and license terms apply to redistributing its output inside a generated app bundle?

---
- **2026-10-08 (Sprint 031)** — `windows.thread.last_error.set_get@0.1.0` accepts a candidate whose GetLastError returns the constant 1234: its one scenario fixes the value at build time. What scenario and relation would make the value unpredictable to a candidate (a run-time value recorded in the trace, judged as "GetLastError returns what SetLastError was given")? Pinned by `test_one_scenario_contract_accepts_a_constant`.
- **2026-10-09 (Sprint 032)** — Answered for the Sprint 031 question above: version 0.2.0 picks three values at run time (QueryPerformanceCounter into a 256-entry table that carries each value's args_sha256) and judges with `exact` (GetLastError returns the argument of the latest SetLastError, checked by hash). The constant is rejected under 0.2.0; `test_constant_rejected_under_0_2_0_accepted_under_0_1_0` holds both verdicts.
- **2026-10-09 (substrate, upstream)** — `substrate.api.Producer` declares its events as `substrate.types.Event` (the bus frame), while the kernel takes and checks the producer's own msgspec Structs. A fix belongs in laffeyp/substrate (`AsyncIterable[Struct]`); w2m bridges it in `w2m.record.producer` until then.
- **2026-10-10 (review 2026-10-10 D8)** — The local VM needs internet when it runs the Steam client so `SteamAPI_Init` succeeds (Decision 2026-10-09, the VM's role), while the guest's lack of internet is "not a defect" for driving it (user, chat 2026-10-09). No sprint has needed Steam in the VM yet (local-VM review L3 open). Raise with the user when one does.
- **2026-10-16 (Sprint 089)** — Does lift() address through R8D-R15D under the 0x67 prefix with 64 bits? `_operand` sets 32-bit addressing only for an E-named base (or an E-named index with no base); the SDM (Vol 1 3.3.7) computes any 32-bit address-size effective address in 32 bits. The class case sets hold 11,202 such cases, 944 with the base's upper half nonzero, and their classes are accepted against Intel's results: evidence against, not proof (both addresses may fault, or reach the same bytes). Settled by examining those 944 cases' accesses, or by a probe on the laptop's x86 processor.

- **2026-10-16 (full review, item 6)** — Milestone C's bound. The review marks plan.md's "frames under 16.7 ms" as stale; the paste cut the text that replaces it. What is milestone C's speed bound (the game's own 113 frames a second on the laptop, 60, or another figure)? — **Answered 2026-10-16 (user):** no bound; as fast as possible, already the user decision of 2026-10-14.
- **2026-10-29 (the defect sweep)** — Which module hosts an `api-ms-win-core-*` set the table does not list? `modules.rs` 351-353 (modules.py 22) give every such set to kernelbase from one Windows row (synch-l1-2-0), while `API_SET_HOSTS` gives api-ms-win-core-com-l1-1-1 to combase.dll, so api-ms-win-core-com-l1-1-0 is likely combase's too. Settled by a LoadLibraryExW and GetModuleFileNameW row per set from the Windows runner, not by asking.
- **2026-10-29 (the threads.rs sweep)** — What stack does Windows give a created thread whose CreateThread commit equals the image's reserve? UnityPlayer.dll's call at reference row 2683 asks dwStackSize 0x100000, the exe's SizeOfStackReserve; Microsoft's Thread Stack Size page makes the reserve the commit rounded up to 1 MB, which would leave no guard page, so the sentence is not the whole answer. Both hosts reserve 0x100000 and clamp the commit to 0x100000 less 5 pages (threads.rs `create_thread`, vm_backend 3111-3112). Settled by a thread_stacks probe row from the Windows runner for a commit equal to the reserve and one above it, not by asking.
- **2026-10-29 (Sprint 218; reviewer-3's Medium 2)** — What TLS index does Windows give a DLL loaded after the start when a DLL imported at the start already holds index 1? The game's case: UnityPlayer.dll holds 1, and steam_api64.dll takes 2 on both hosts by the frozen host's rule (vm_backend 3959). `contracts/windows.loader.late_tls` holds only a program with no other TLS module, index 1, and Microsoft's PE format page says only that the loader writes the index at AddressOfIndex. Settled by a late_tls probe row from the Windows runner: a program importing a TLS DLL at the start that loads a second TLS DLL and reads its index, not by asking.
- **2026-10-29 (reviewer-3's project review, Medium 3)** — Where does v0.2's product slice go in the plan? v0.2 §1 and §12.1 make the product the one-command operation (resolve the Steam distribution, obtain it under the user's account, prepare it, establish its Steam session, run, preserve state, relaunch), §4.2 the Steam bridge a first-class subsystem, §4.3 the output a Mac application bundle; `docs/rust-port-plan-2026-10-24.md` runs steps 1-10 to milestone C with no step for them, and nothing records the deferral. The plan is the user's; the question is theirs: a step 11 after milestone C, a thread beside steps 5-10, or another place. — **Answered 2026-10-29 (user):** more milestones, after C, unless something must change now; nothing must, by lookup. The Decision "Milestones for the product slice, after milestone C" gives milestones D (the product slice) and E (the Steam session).
- **2026-10-29 (reviewer-3's refreshed project review, direction)** — Milestone E's gate (plan step 12) holds the generated steamclient64.dll to a capture of the game's Steam calls made on Windows. The Windows laptop is expected, not yet here. Is the capture made on the laptop, or by the Windows runner the family probes use? Revisit when milestone D's gate is met, or on the laptop's arrival, whichever comes first.

### Sprint 279 (2026-10-31, closed)
- **Scope:** reviewer-3's Lows 1 and 3 at 278: the view's copies made relaxed atomics, so a guest thread writing a buffer the host copies gives torn bytes, not a data race; the design's table of the structures the host reaches, with each one's access and ordering; views built from a slice counted
- **Dual contract:** signal — none, an access path held by tests; artifact pass (`scripts/gates.sh` exit 0 in 670 s, kept in `store/gates/passes.tsv` as sprint 279 at 54a6d4c01 with root=358439357 and "rust=crates python=0 samples=0", rdeps(w2m_vm), 156 tests, base=77d21ab2f, Sprint 278's close; multi-vCPU: not run (Python gates not run)); observation pass (every length 0 to 40 at every offset 0 to 15 read back; 20,000 copies racing 20,000 writes read only bytes written; 156 `w2m_vm` tests before the commit; plant 1, "length 1 at offset 0 wrote outside its bytes"; plant 2, "cases.rs: 2 views built from a slice, census D7 allows 1")
- **CI does not run:** `store_pair_release`'s test (aarch64); CI runs the copies' tests and the count tests on Linux.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:** *Sequence narration:* no record. *Observation 1 (a design's premise that did not hold):* 276's choice 2 allowed plain copies of bytes "no running vCPU writes at the time"; a guest buffer a host request names is not such bytes, and under Rust's model a racing plain copy is undefined, not torn; RFC 3301 (open, not merged) and C++'s P1478 give the remedy, relaxed atomic copies. Disposition: resolved-here, choice 2 amended. *Observation 2 (a defect the table found):* `x86_address_at` reads the reconstruction table's count plain and then its entries; on the host's Arm ordering an entry read may pass the count read on two vCPUs. Disposition: the table's row names the card that moves `run.rs`, which reads the count Acquire. *Observation 3 (a plant that could not fire):* the card's plant 1, the word loop stopping a word early, leaves the result unchanged, the byte loop copying the rest; replaced by a byte-order fault. Disposition: recorded, as the result says. *Observation 4 (the review split in two cards):* the review's Low 3 came with `host_request.rs`'s move in view; the copy semantics are a concept of their own, so `host_request.rs` is card 280. Disposition: resolved-here. *Observation 5 (edits):* the card by Write; the copies, the head and Sync comments, the tests, the design's amendment, table and corrections, the census, Built 269, this entry and the result by the Edit tool; the count test rewritten by Write; the plants by one-line `sed`s restored by `cp` and checked by `cmp`; the card's work commit, gates line and status one `sed` substitution each; the tail roll on both sides by the Edit tool, verified by `diff`; Sprint 278's CI row by `printf`. Disposition: recorded, no breach.
